Mulch and fencing support this zone but do not define its actual protected extent.
  45. The correct answer is A (In the general notes and specifications, with responsibility for monitoring clearly assigned): a multi-year monitoring obligation must be documented in a location that survives project closeout, such as the general notes or specifications, with clear assignment of responsibility. Leaving it undocumented risks the requirement being forgotten once construction ends.
  46. The correct answer is C (Assess the extent of damage with the arborist and determine necessary remediation): even with fencing installed correctly, unexpected root damage can occur. The appropriate response is a professional assessment to determine the tree's prognosis and any remediation needed, rather than ignoring the issue or taking disproportionate contractual action.
  47. The correct answer is D (Showing the fence location graphically helps the contractor and inspector verify field placement): a graphic note on the plan gives a specific, verifiable location for erosion control measures that a specification alone cannot convey. This supports both installation accuracy and inspection during construction.
  48. The correct answers are A and B: regulatory agencies reviewing a mitigation plan typically expect documented replacement planting quantities and species along with a monitoring and maintenance schedule confirming survival. Contractor markup and unrelated client budget items are financial matters unrelated to demonstrating environmental mitigation compliance.
  49. The correct answer is B (Without clear handling instructions, the sculpture is at risk of damage and the bid may be inconsistent): valuable or sensitive items being relocated need documented handling and storage guidance so bidders price the work consistently and the item is protected. Assuming contractors will handle it appropriately without direction creates unnecessary risk.
  50. The correct answer is A (A fixed building line provides a stable reference that reduces cumulative layout error): dimensioning from a stable, unmoving reference like a building face minimizes the compounding of small errors that can occur when referencing other movable or less precise elements. This approach improves field accuracy for the contractor.
  51. The correct answer is C (Ambiguous or duplicate hatching can cause the contractor to install the wrong paving material): distinct materials must be clearly differentiated on the plan so field crews can correctly identify what to install where. Relying solely on the contractor to cross-reference specifications increases the risk of installation errors.
  52. The correct answer is D (Relocate the fountain so the accessible route maintains its required unobstructed clear width): an accessible route must maintain its required clear width free of obstructions along its full length, and this requirement is not limited to ramps. Relocating the fountain resolves the conflict while preserving both the amenity and required accessibility.
  53. The correct answer is A (Specialty Unit Pavers): the specific pattern, coursing, and joint layout of a specialty paver assembly is communicated to bidders and field crews primarily through plan symbols keyed to a legend. If the legend does not accurately match the symbols shown, contractors cannot reliably interpret which paver type or pattern belongs where, undermining the reliability of the entire plan. A QA/QC review should catch this mismatch and require the legend to be corrected so it matches every symbol used throughout the drawing set. The layers beneath the pavers are less dependent on legend accuracy since they are typically described in specifications and general notes.
  54. The correct answer is D (Coordinate relocation of either the tree grate or the utility to resolve the vertical conflict): a vertical conflict between a tree grate structure and an underground utility must be resolved through coordination before construction, since installing the grate as designed could damage the utility or compromise the tree pit.
  55. The correct answer is C (Concrete Footing): the footing carries the bench's dead load and any lateral or overturning forces down to the subgrade, and its size, depth, and reinforcement must match the manufacturer's mounting specifications for that particular bench model. A manufacturer name alone tells the contractor what product to buy but not how to install and coordinate it with the footing, anchor bolts, and surrounding paving. Without dimensions and mounting details, the footing could be undersized or misaligned with the bench's actual anchor pattern. This is why site furnishings require more than a product name to be construction-ready.
  56. The correct answers are A and B: verifying the material legend matches actual plan hatching and confirming dimension strings are complete against a clear baseline are core consistency checks for a layout and materials plan. Client preferences for unrelated items and contractor profit percentages are not part of a technical QA/QC review.
  57. The correct answer is A (Specialty Paving Pattern (Wearing Course)): a specialty pattern involves specific joint lines, unit coursing, and material transitions at the wearing surface that a small-scale materials plan cannot show in enough detail to build from. A keyed reference to a detail sheet gives the contractor the exact layout information needed to install the pattern correctly. Without that cross-reference, the contractor is left without the specific geometry needed, even though the underlying bedding, base, and subgrade layers can often be described adequately in general notes. Coordinating this reference is a basic construction documentation task during multidisciplinary review.
  58. The correct answer is D (The contractor may place benches inconsistently since exact spacing and alignment are not defined): relying only on overall dimensions without locating individual furnishings leaves too much room for interpretation, potentially resulting in uneven spacing or misalignment that does not match the designer's intent.
  59. The correct answer is C (The discrepancy between the plan quantity and the schedule quantity needs to be resolved before bidding): a mismatch between the plan and the schedule creates ambiguity about actual scope, which can lead to inconsistent bid pricing. QA/QC review should catch and resolve this type of discrepancy prior to issuance.
  60. The correct answers are A and B: confirming footings do not conflict with underground utilities and confirming accessible clearances around furnishings are essential coordination checks for a layout plan. Supplier shipping logistics and internal invoice status are administrative matters unrelated to the technical coordination of the construction documents.
  61. The correct answer is C (Open-Graded Base Reservoir Course): this layer stores stormwater beneath the permeable pavers and is entirely hidden once construction is complete, so contractors have no way to confirm its required aggregate gradation or thickness except through the plan's abbreviation and legend. Undefined abbreviations create ambiguity that can lead to inconsistent bids, since one contractor may price a different material or depth than another interprets from the same undefined term. Every abbreviation used on a plan should be defined in the legend or general notes so all bidders and field personnel interpret it consistently. Surface layers like the pavers themselves are easier to verify visually, but this buried reservoir course depends entirely on clear documentation.
  62. The correct answer is B (Adjust the bollard spacing and location to satisfy the required fire lane clearance): fire lane clearance requirements apply broadly to obstructions within the required lane, not just gates, so the bollard layout must be revised to comply before the plan can be finalized and permitted.
  63. The correct answer is B (A schedule consolidates specification references, colors, and finishes in one easily checked location): a materials schedule organizes detailed information in a format that is easier to verify for completeness and consistency than scattered plan labels alone, supporting more reliable QA/QC review and bidding.
  64. The correct answer is A (A clear setback dimension avoids field conflicts and ensures accessible route width is maintained): without a documented setback, the water feature could encroach on the path and reduce the required clear width of an accessible route. A specific dimension removes ambiguity for the contractor during layout.
  65. The correct answer is C (Setting the root ball slightly high accounts for settling and helps prevent the trunk flare from being buried): backfill and surrounding soil tend to settle over time, so planting slightly high compensates for this and helps ensure the trunk flare remains visible and unburied, which supports long-term tree health.
  66. The correct answer is D (Mulch depth should be reduced to about two to three inches and pulled away from the trunk): excessive mulch piled against the trunk, sometimes called volcano mulching, can promote rot, pests, and girdling roots. A depth of about two to three inches kept clear of the trunk is the appropriate correction.
  67. The correct answer is A (Reconcile the conflicting ratios so the detail and general notes match exactly): inconsistent backfill mix ratios between the detail and the general notes create ambiguity about the correct specification, which could result in improperly amended soil. The documents should be reconciled to show one consistent requirement.
  68. The correct answer is B (Specifying material and removal timing prevents girdling and ensures stakes are removed once no longer needed): staking left in place too long or made of an inappropriate material can girdle a young tree as it grows. Clear material and removal timing requirements protect the tree's long-term health.
  69. The correct answer is D (The discrepancy between the schedule spacing and the graphic spacing needs to be corrected): a mismatch between the written spacing and the graphic representation creates confusion about the intended planting density. QA/QC review should identify and resolve this type of inconsistency before the documents are issued.
  70. The correct answer is C (Without specified depth and material, the drainage layer may be under-built, leading to waterlogged roots): a drainage layer that is undersized or built with the wrong material may fail to adequately move excess water away from the growing medium, risking root rot in a raised planter application.
  71. The correct answer is A (Update the detail to specify a strapping method that minimizes trunk damage while providing adequate support): wire and hose guying can cut into bark and cause lasting damage as a tree grows, so updating the detail to a wider strapping method reduces this risk while still providing needed support.
  72. The correct answers are A, B, and C: root ball depth and trunk flare treatment, backfill soil mix and compaction, and mulch type and depth kept clear of the trunk are all standard elements of a tree planting detail. Irrigation controller warranty terms belong in the specifications for that system, not a planting detail.
  73. The correct answer is D (Adequate soil volume is essential for tree health and longevity, especially in confined urban planting areas): trees in constrained settings like parking lot islands often struggle without sufficient soil volume to support healthy root development, so documenting a minimum requirement supports long-term tree survival.
  74. The correct answer is B (Without amendment or preparation guidance, poor drainage in clay soil may lead to plant failure): heavy clay soils often drain poorly and can cause root rot in perennials if not properly amended or prepared. Leaving this guidance out of the detail creates risk of plant failure after installation.
  75. The correct answer is C (Reconcile the bed edges between the two plans so they match consistently): conflicting bed edge lines between the planting and irrigation plans create ambiguity about the actual planting area boundary, which could result in improperly zoned irrigation coverage. The two plans should be reconciled before issuance.
  76. The correct answer is A (Coordinated details ensure the planting and structural elements work together without conflicting requirements): a green screen system depends on both the structural support and the plant material working together, so the two details must be coordinated to avoid conflicting dimensions, attachment points, or growing conditions.
  77. The correct answer is B (Confirm plant selections are appropriate for the actual ponding depth and duration shown in the grading plan): plant tolerance to wet conditions varies significantly, so the selected species must match the actual ponding depth and duration engineered into the rain garden, not just a general wet-dry tolerance assumption.
  78. The correct answer is D (Unapproved substitutions can compromise design intent, plant health suitability, and code compliance): allowing substitutions without landscape architect review risks introducing species that are unsuitable for the site conditions, inconsistent with design intent, or noncompliant with any required plant list, so approval should remain with the designer.
  79. The correct answer is C (Proper timing and establishment watering are critical to plant survival and are not implied by spacing alone): plant spacing alone does not communicate when planting should occur or how establishment watering should be managed, both of which significantly affect survival rates in a bioswale environment.
  80. The correct answers are A and B: adequate soil volume with structural soil or suspended pavement systems, and coordination of grate or opening size with future trunk diameter, are essential considerations for urban tree pits in paved contexts. Selecting the fastest-growing species or matching grate finish to signage are not technical planting considerations.
  81. The correct answer is C (Taller segmental walls typically require geogrid reinforcement to resist lateral earth pressure safely): as retained height increases, lateral earth pressure grows substantially, and unreinforced segmental walls may not have sufficient resistance. Geogrid reinforcement should be referenced and coordinated with engineering for taller walls.
  82. The correct answer is B (Gravity walls rely on mass for stability and may need engineering review for taller walls): gravity walls depend entirely on their own weight to resist overturning and sliding, which becomes more critical as height increases or soil conditions are less favorable, warranting engineering review for this condition.
  83. The correct answer is D (Without a base course and subgrade prep, pavement is likely to fail prematurely under load): a surface course alone cannot adequately distribute loads or manage moisture without a properly prepared base and subgrade beneath it, leading to rutting, cracking, or other premature pavement failure.
  84. The correct answer is A (The base course distributes traffic loads and provides a stable, well-drained platform beneath the surface): the base course is a structural layer that spreads loads over a wider area and provides drainage beneath the wearing surface, both of which are essential to pavement performance and longevity.
  85. The correct answer is A (Without control joints, the slab is prone to random cracking from shrinkage and thermal movement): concrete naturally shrinks as it cures and moves with temperature changes, so control joints are needed to direct cracking to predictable locations rather than allowing random, unsightly cracks across the slab.
  86. The correct answer is C (Reinforcement at the wrong depth reduces its effectiveness at controlling cracking within the slab): reinforcement placed on the subgrade rather than held at the correct depth within the slab loses much of its ability to resist cracking forces, undermining the intended structural performance of the pavement.
  87. The correct answer is D (Control joint spacing helps manage shrinkage cracking along the length of the wall): even non-structural elements like cast-in-place seat walls are subject to shrinkage as concrete cures, so control joints at appropriate spacing help direct cracking to controlled locations rather than random ones.
  88. The correct answers are A and B: a typical flexible pavement section places compacted subgrade at the bottom, an aggregate base course above it, and the asphalt surface course on top. Waterproofing membranes and root barriers are not standard components of a shared use path pavement section.
  89. The correct answer is B (Coordinate conduit routing and sleeve locations between disciplines to avoid field conflicts): electrical conduit for a fountain must be coordinated with the basin structure so sleeves and routing are planned before construction, avoiding costly field conflicts or damage to either system.
  90. The correct answer is A (Without a waterproofing membrane and drainage layer, the roof structure is at serious risk of water damage): a green roof assembly depends on a continuous waterproofing membrane and functioning drainage layer to protect the underlying structure from water infiltration and ponding, both of which are essential to the system.
  91. The correct answer is C (The root barrier must be above the waterproofing membrane to prevent root penetration from damaging it): the root barrier's function is to keep plant roots from reaching and puncturing the waterproofing membrane, so it must be positioned above the membrane, not below it, to serve its intended purpose.
  92. The correct answer is B (Coordinate with the civil engineer to confirm the trench drain outlet ties properly into the storm system): a trench drain must discharge somewhere, and confirming its connection to the storm sewer system with the civil engineer ensures the drainage detail will actually function as intended once installed.
  93. The correct answer is D (An undersized grate can restrict flow and cause surface ponding around the drain): a grate opening smaller than the pipe beneath it creates a bottleneck that can restrict water flow into the drain, resulting in surface ponding rather than the intended efficient drainage.
  94. The correct answer is A (It relieves hydrostatic pressure behind the wall by collecting and conveying subsurface water away): a French drain behind a retaining wall collects groundwater that would otherwise build up hydrostatic pressure against the wall, conveying it away and reducing the risk of wall failure or excessive moisture.
  95. The correct answer is D (Footings that do not extend below frost depth can heave and cause structural movement over time): in regions with freezing winters, footings above frost depth are subject to frost heave, which can shift or crack the structure over repeated freeze-thaw cycles, so frost depth must inform footing design.
  96. The correct answers are A, B, and C: a continuous waterproofing membrane, a root barrier, and a drainage layer are essential components of a typical green roof assembly above the structural deck. A vapor barrier identical to an interior wall assembly is not a standard or accurate description of a green roof layer.
  97. The correct answer is C (Add the required clearance dimension to the detail to ensure code compliance): outdoor fireplaces are subject to fire code clearance requirements just as interior installations are, so the required clearance to combustibles must be documented on the detail to ensure a compliant, safe installation.
  98. The correct answer is B (The elevation conflict between the detail and the grading plan needs to be resolved before issuance): a catch basin set higher than the surrounding grade cannot function correctly to collect surface runoff, so this elevation conflict must be identified and corrected before the documents are finalized.
  99. The correct answer is D (A protection board shields the waterproofing membrane from puncture during backfilling and root growth): backfilling a planter with growing medium and later root growth can puncture an unprotected waterproofing membrane, so a protection board is an important addition to preserve the membrane's integrity over time.
  100. The correct answers are A and B: wall heights exceeding common unreinforced limits and the presence of surcharge loads such as adjacent parking or foundations both typically trigger the need for additional geotechnical engineering input. Wall color and standard cap dimensions are aesthetic or catalog details unrelated to structural design needs.
  101. The correct answer is A (Vehicular loading generally requires a thicker base course than pedestrian-only pavement to prevent failure): vehicles impose significantly greater loads than pedestrians, so a vehicular pavement section generally needs a thicker base course to adequately support that loading and prevent premature failure.
  102. The correct answer is B (A base plate and anchor bolt layout coordinated with the structural engineer's design): steel columns require a proper base plate and anchor bolt connection to transfer loads safely into the footing, and this connection should be coordinated with and confirmed by the structural engineer of record.
  103. The correct answer is C (A defined overflow elevation ensures the system functions as intended and coordinates with the grading design): without a specific overflow elevation tied to the grading plan's ponding depth, the bioretention system may overflow too early or too late, undermining its intended stormwater management function.
  104. The correct answers are A and B: reinforcing steel size, spacing, and cover, along with footing width, depth, and bearing requirements, are essential structural information for a cast-in-place concrete wall section. Pricing information and subcontractor preferences are administrative matters that do not belong on a technical wall detail.
  105. The correct answer is C (Expansion joints require a compressible filler to accommodate thermal movement without cracking the slab): expansion joints must allow adjacent slab sections to move independently as they expand and contract with temperature changes, which requires a compressible filler rather than a rigid material like mortar.
  106. The correct answer is B (Proper aggregate gradation ensures effective water movement to the pipe while filtering out fine sediment): the aggregate surrounding a subsurface drain pipe must be sized appropriately to allow water to move freely toward the pipe while still filtering out fine soil particles that could clog the system.
  107. The correct answer is A (A defined coating system and performance expectations to ensure long-term corrosion protection): an unspecified coating leaves durability and appearance to chance, so defining the specific coating system and expected service life ensures the metal arbor receives adequate long-term corrosion protection.
  108. The correct answer is D (Without specified anchorage, the veneer may not be adequately secured and could fail over time): stone veneer applied without a defined bonding method or mechanical anchorage risks detaching from the substrate over time, particularly under exterior weathering conditions, so the connection method must be specified.
  109. The correct answer is B (Confirm the drainage layer connects to roof drains or scuppers that convey water off the structure): a drainage layer alone does not remove water from the roof; it must connect to a functioning outlet such as a roof drain or scupper to actually convey excess water off the structure.
  110. The correct answer is D (Light pole and fixture locations must avoid conflicts with trees, furnishings, and underground utilities): lighting elements are physical objects that occupy space above and below grade, so their locations must be coordinated with the landscape layout to prevent conflicts with plantings, furnishings, or utilities.
  111. The correct answer is A (Coordinate with the irrigation consultant to reroute the mainline away from the tree's root zone): an irrigation mainline running through a tree's root ball location would likely damage roots during trenching, so coordination to reroute the line is necessary to protect the tree while still serving the irrigation system.
  112. The correct answers are A and B: verifying irrigation components do not conflict with paving, furnishings, or trees, and confirming consistent bed lines between plans, are essential coordination steps. Contractor lunch schedules and unrelated interior finish selections have no bearing on technical coordination between disciplines.
  113. The correct answer is C (Confirming required use zones and fall surfacing extents around each piece of equipment): playground safety depends heavily on adequate use zones and appropriate fall surfacing extending the required distance around each piece of equipment, making this the most critical safety-related coordination item.
  114. The correct answer is C (Confirm sign foundation locations and heights do not conflict with plantings, grading, or accessible routes): wayfinding signs have physical foundations and heights that must be checked against surrounding site conditions to avoid conflicts with plant material, grading, or required accessible route clearances.
  115. The correct answer is A (Work with the electrical engineer to route conduit around sensitive root zones where feasible): coordinating conduit routing with the electrical engineer to avoid dense root systems where possible helps protect existing plant material while still allowing the lighting system to be properly installed.
  116. The correct answer is D (An incomplete legend can cause bidders to misinterpret plan information and submit inconsistent bids): every abbreviation used repeatedly on the drawings should be included in the legend so bidders interpret the information consistently, reducing the risk of misunderstanding or inconsistent pricing.
  117. The correct answer is B (Standardize symbols across all sheets so they match the legend consistently throughout the set): inconsistent symbol usage across sheets undermines the reliability of the legend and can confuse bidders and field crews, so QA/QC review should require consistent symbol usage throughout the entire set.
  118. The correct answer is A (Without size and spacing information, contractors cannot accurately price or install the groundcover): container size and spacing directly affect both material cost and installation labor, so omitting them from the schedule leaves contractors without the information needed to accurately bid and install the groundcover.
  119. The correct answer is C (Without a specific manufacturer and product number, substitutions may not match the intended appearance): color names vary between manufacturers and product lines, so referencing only a color name without a specific manufacturer and product number risks an installed product that does not match the designer's intended appearance.
  120. The correct answers are A and B: general notes typically address applicable codes, permits, and reference specifications, along with coordination responsibilities and verification of existing conditions across the whole project. Preconstruction meeting refreshments and individual bidder markup percentages are not appropriate content for general notes.
  121. The correct answer is D (Align irrigation zones with planting hydrozones so plants with similar water needs are grouped together): grouping plants by water need into matching irrigation zones, known as hydrozoning, allows the system to deliver appropriate amounts of water to each area, supporting both plant health and water efficiency.
  122. The correct answer is B (A cross-reference ensures the contractor can locate installation requirements for each scheduled sign type): a sign schedule listing types and quantities without a reference to the corresponding installation detail leaves the contractor without the specific mounting and footing information needed to properly install each sign.
  123. The correct answer is B (That the specified surfacing material and depth provide adequate impact attenuation for the equipment's critical fall height): playground surfacing must be matched to the critical fall height of the specific equipment it surrounds, since inadequate depth or an unsuitable material could fail to sufficiently cushion a fall.
  124. The correct answer is C (Identifying specific code editions removes ambiguity and ensures consistent interpretation during permitting and construction): code requirements can change meaningfully between editions, so a general reference without a specific edition leaves room for inconsistent interpretation during plan review, permitting, and construction.
  125. The correct answer is D (Adjust the grading or path alignment so the running slope meets the required accessible maximum): an accessible route must maintain a running slope within the required maximum to remain usable and compliant, so the grading or alignment should be revised rather than leaving the excessive slope in place.
  126. The correct answer is A (Cross slope on an accessible route has a lower maximum, so five percent likely exceeds it): accessible route cross slope is typically limited to a maximum well below five percent, so a ramp detail showing this dimension across its width likely fails to meet the applicable accessibility standard.
  127. The correct answer is A (Revise the stall dimensions to meet or exceed the local zoning ordinance's minimum requirement): parking stall dimensions are commonly regulated by local zoning ordinances, so a plan showing undersized stalls should be corrected to meet or exceed the applicable minimum before it can be approved.
  128. The correct answers are A and B: accessible route slope maximums and required setback distances from property lines are common code and standards items that require verification on a site plan. Table typeface choices and meeting scheduling preferences have no relevance to code or accessibility compliance review.
  129. The correct answer is D (Revise the handrail height to meet or exceed the minimum required by the applicable code): handrail height requirements generally apply to exterior stairways just as they do to interior ones, so a handrail below the required minimum must be corrected to comply with the applicable building code.
  130. The correct answer is C (Reconcile the discrepancy so the plan and detail show a consistent wall thickness): a mismatched wall thickness between the plan and its detail creates ambiguity about the actual intended dimension, so the discrepancy must be resolved to a single consistent value before the documents are issued.
  131. The correct answer is B (To catch coordination errors, omissions, and inconsistencies before the documents reach the public bidding stage): a peer review is intended to identify technical problems in the drawing set while there is still time to correct them, reducing the risk of costly errors surfacing after bidding or during construction.
  132. The correct answer is C (Clarify which detail governs and issue a formal written response or bulletin documenting the resolution): a request for information about conflicting details deserves a clear, documented resolution so the contractor has an official record of which detail to follow, avoiding future disputes or inconsistent construction.
  133. The correct answer is D (Reject or request revision of the shop drawing and require it to conform to the approved design intent): shop drawings must be reviewed against the approved construction documents, and any dimensional discrepancy should be corrected through revision rather than accepted or left to be resolved after fabrication.
  134. The correct answer is A (To ensure the contractor can locate every referenced detail and avoid confusion during construction): a detail callout that references a nonexistent detail leaves the contractor without the information they were directed to find, so verifying every callout has a matching detail is an important QA/QC step.
  135. The correct answer is B (Request clarification from the bidder to confirm the price reflects the full scope described in the specifications): an unusually low unit price could indicate a scope misunderstanding or pricing error, so requesting clarification helps confirm the bidder correctly understood the required scope before award decisions are made.
  136. The correct answer is B (The conflict between the specification and the drawing needs to be identified and resolved before bidding): when the specifications and drawings describe different materials for the same element, the conflict creates ambiguity for bidders and must be resolved so both documents agree before the project goes out to bid.
  137. The correct answer is C (Document the nonconformance and require correction consistent with the approved construction documents): an installed condition that does not match the approved detail is a nonconformance that should be documented and corrected during construction, rather than accepted informally or addressed only after the warranty period.
  138. The correct answer is A (Division 31 typically covers earthwork while Division 32 covers exterior improvements such as paving and planting): under common specification organization, earthwork generally falls under Division 31, while Division 32 addresses exterior improvements like paving, planting, and site furnishings, helping bidders locate the correct requirements.
  139. The correct answer is D (To ensure the sum of individual dimensions matches overall dimensions, preventing field layout errors): verifying that dimension strings mathematically close confirms internal consistency within the drawing, which helps prevent field layout errors that could otherwise arise from an uncaught dimensioning mistake.
  140. The correct answer is D (It allows the owner to compare a base bid against optional upgraded scope before committing to added cost): a bid alternate gives the owner flexibility to evaluate the cost impact of an upgrade before deciding whether to include it, supporting informed budget decisions during the bidding process.
  141. The correct answer is C (Whether the proposed substitute meets the performance, appearance, and durability criteria of the specified product): a substitution review should focus on whether the proposed product genuinely performs and appears equivalent to what was specified, protecting design intent and long-term performance rather than cost alone.
  142. The correct answer is B (Document the deficiency on a punch list and require correction before final completion is granted): a mulch depth deficiency compared to the approved detail is a legitimate punch list item that should be corrected before final completion, not deferred indefinitely or dismissed as insignificant.
  143. The correct answer is A (Review the existing conditions documentation and field verify the claim before recommending action to the owner): a claimed unforeseen condition should be verified against the existing conditions documentation and field observation before the landscape architect makes any recommendation to the owner regarding the change order.
  144. The correct answers are A and B: responding to bidder questions with addenda and reviewing bid tabulations for completeness are core bidding phase responsibilities. Selecting finishes for an unrelated renovation and preparing an original design development concept belong to entirely different, earlier, or unrelated phases of work.
  145. The correct answer is D (Organizing costs by standard divisions helps ensure consistent, comparable estimates and clear bid scope): using a recognized specification structure to organize cost estimates provides consistency and allows for clearer comparison between estimates and bids, supporting more accurate budgeting throughout the project.
  146. The correct answer is A (Document the nonconformance, notify the contractor and owner, and coordinate remediation with the arborist): root damage from trenching within an approved protection zone is a nonconformance that should be documented and addressed promptly, including coordination with the arborist to assess and remediate any harm to the tree.
  147. The correct answer is C (It prevents unscheduled plant symbols from creating ambiguity about species, quantity, or specification for bidders): every plant symbol shown on the plan should correspond to a schedule entry so bidders have complete species, quantity, and specification information for every plant shown, avoiding pricing errors.
  148. The correct answers are A and B: cross-checking dimensions and details for consistency and confirming code compliance items such as accessible slopes and setbacks are legitimate, substantive QA/QC checklist items. Office supply restocking and visitor parking arrangements are administrative matters unrelated to technical document review.
  149. The correct answer is B (To verify that the requested payment amount reasonably reflects the percentage of work actually completed): payment application review exists to confirm the contractor is being paid fairly and accurately for work actually performed to date, protecting the owner from overpayment relative to progress.
  150. The correct answer is B (Either the legend has an unused entry, or trees to remain were not indicated on the plan): if a defined legend symbol never appears on the corresponding plan, either the legend contains unnecessary content or the plan is missing information it should show, and this discrepancy should be investigated.
  151. The correct answer is C (Request additional documentation or testing data confirming the product meets the specified requirement): a submittal that does not clearly demonstrate compliance with a stated performance requirement should not be approved as-is; additional data should be requested to confirm the product actually meets the specification.
  152. The correct answers are A and B: a final punch list confirming corrected items and record or as-built drawings reflecting actual constructed conditions are standard project closeout deliverables. A new concept plan for a future phase or an original competition entry are unrelated to closing out the current construction contract.
  153. The correct answer is D (A field walkthrough comparing installed conditions against the approved plans, details, and specifications): confirming that a completed installation actually matches the construction documents requires a direct field comparison against the approved plans, details, and specifications, not reliance on verbal assurance or paperwork alone.
  154. The correct answer is A (To minimize ambiguity, reduce change orders, and support accurate contractor bidding and construction): the overarching goal of final internal consistency review across notes, schedules, legends, plans, and details is to reduce ambiguity and errors that could otherwise lead to change orders, disputes, or construction mistakes.
  155. The correct answer is D (Design-bid-build, where design is fully complete before competitive bidding begins): It calls for a fully designed project competitively bid to prequalified general contractors at a fixed price. That sequence, complete design before bidding, is the defining feature of design-bid-build. Design-build and CM-at-risk both involve the builder earlier in the design process. Integrated project delivery uses a shared-risk multiparty agreement, which is not described here.
  156. The correct answer is D (Design-build, where one contracted entity performs both design and construction): A single contracted entity holding both design and construction responsibility eliminates the split liability common between separate designer and builder contracts. Design-bid-build and multiple prime contracting both keep design and construction under separate contracts. CM-agency also keeps the construction manager as an advisor rather than a single responsible party.
  157. The correct answer is D (CM-at-risk, where the construction manager holds the construction contract): Early construction manager involvement for estimating, constructability review, and an eventual guaranteed maximum price describes CM-at-risk, where the construction manager holds the actual construction contract. Design-bid-build selects a contractor after design is complete based on price. Design-build-operate adds long-term operations, which is not mentioned. Job order contracting uses pre-priced tasks for smaller recurring work.
  158. The correct answer is B (CM-at-risk allows earlier contractor input but limits competitive low-bid pricing): CM-at-risk brings the contractor in early for cost input and constructability review but typically negotiates a guaranteed maximum price rather than relying purely on open competitive low-bid pricing. Design-bid-build does not always cost less, since it forgoes early contractor input. Both methods retain a role for the landscape architect during construction observation.
  159. The correct answers are A and B: One entity being responsible for both design and construction, and the ability to overlap design and construction phases, are genuine hallmarks of design-build. The owner does not contract separately with an unrelated builder under this method, since that describes design-bid-build instead. Design-build still allows change orders during construction like any delivery method.
  160. The correct answer is D (It ensures the apparent low bidder will enter into the contract if awarded): A bid bond guarantees that if the bidder is awarded the contract, it will enter into the agreement and furnish the required performance and payment bonds. It does not guarantee project completion, protect subcontractors from nonpayment, or insure against defective materials, which are the roles of other bonds or contract provisions.
  161. The correct answer is B (The contractor's failure to complete the work according to the contract): A performance bond protects the owner if the contractor fails to complete the work in accordance with the contract, since the surety becomes responsible for completion or costs. It does not address mechanic's liens, bid collusion, or the landscape architect's professional liability, which are separate risks covered by other instruments.
  162. The correct answer is D (Issue a written addendum distributed to every bidder of record): A clarification that affects the bidding documents and could influence pricing must be issued as a written addendum distributed to every bidder of record, keeping the competitive process fair. An informal phone call to one bidder, waiting until construction, or updating files without notice would give some bidders an unfair informational advantage.
  163. The correct answer is D (To allow bidders to ask questions and clarify project conditions before submitting bids): A pre-bid meeting exists to let prospective bidders ask questions and clarify site conditions, access, and scope before finalizing their bids. It occurs before bids are due, so it cannot finalize a guaranteed maximum price, award a contract, or negotiate individual unit prices, which would undermine open competitive bidding.
  164. The correct answers are A, C, and D: The invitation to bid and instructions to bidders, the bid form and owner-contractor agreement, and the general and supplementary conditions are all standard front-end, Division 00 documents that establish bidding and contractual procedures. Detailed technical execution requirements for planting installation belong in the technical specification sections, not the front-end documents.
  165. The correct answer is A (A bid protest filed according to the procedures in the bidding documents): A formal bid protest, following the procedures established in the bidding documents, is the correct mechanism for a bidder to challenge an award based on an alleged failure to meet requirements. A construction change directive and RFI apply during construction administration, and a substitution request concerns products, not bid eligibility disputes.
  166. The correct answers are A and C: A payment bond guarantees that subcontractors and suppliers will be paid for labor and materials, protecting them if the general contractor defaults, and it is commonly required together with a performance bond on public work. It does not insure the landscape architect against design claims or replace the contractor's general liability insurance.
  167. The correct answer is C (To let the owner adjust project scope up or down based on available funding): Add-alternates let the owner scale project scope up or down depending on how the base bid pricing compares to available funding, providing budget flexibility. They do not eliminate the bid bond requirement, remove the need for technical specifications, or restrict consideration to only the lowest base bid amount.

Practice Test 5 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (It establishes a pre-agreed rate for adjusting cost if quantities vary): Unit pricing establishes a pre-agreed rate per unit of measure so the contract price can be fairly adjusted if actual quantities, such as rock excavation, differ from the estimate. It does not fix the total price without accounting for quantity, remove the excavation obligation, or eliminate the schedule of values.
  2. The correct answer is B (A formal substitution request demonstrating the product is equivalent to the specified basis): A substitution request requires the contractor to demonstrate that the proposed product is genuinely equivalent to the specified basis of design in relevant performance and quality respects. A verbal request, a unilateral construction change directive from the architect, or an undocumented schedule of values change would not satisfy this requirement.
  3. The correct answer is A (CSI MasterFormat, which organizes specifications by division and section number): CSI MasterFormat organizes construction specifications into a standardized sequence of numbered divisions and sections used industry-wide, allowing consistent organization across projects. Building code occupancy classifications, drawing scale conventions, and federal-only guide specifications serve different purposes and do not provide this general specification organization system.
  4. The correct answer is D (General, Products, and Execution, describing requirements in that sequence): SectionFormat divides each individual specification section into three parts, General, Products, and Execution, addressing administrative requirements, material requirements, and installation requirements in that order. Bidding requirements and contract forms belong to the front-end documents, not an individual technical section, and payment applications are a separate construction administration process.
  5. The correct answer is A (A reference standard specification incorporating an established industry document): Citing an established industry document such as ASTM C94 rather than restating its requirements is the defining feature of a reference standard specification, which incorporates the standard by reference. This differs from proprietary specifications naming manufacturers, performance specifications describing outcomes, and descriptive specifications spelling out every material and method directly.
  6. The correct answers are A, B, and C: Proprietary specifications naming manufacturers, performance specifications stating required outcomes, and descriptive specifications detailing materials and methods directly are all recognized specification types a landscape architect may choose among for technical sections. A bid protest specification is not a real specification type; a bid protest is a bidding dispute procedure.
  7. The correct answer is B (A performance specification defining the required result rather than the method): Defining a required distribution uniformity outcome and letting any manufacturer demonstrate compliance describes a performance specification, which focuses on the desired result rather than a specific method or product. A proprietary or closed specification would restrict bidders to one product, and a reference standard would cite a testing procedure rather than a performance outcome.
  8. The correct answer is B (Administrative requirements such as submittal procedures and project meetings): Division 01 contains administrative and procedural general requirements, such as submittal procedures and project meeting requirements, that apply across the whole project rather than to one material category. Detailed planting procedures, product data sheets, and the bid form and invitation to bid belong in the technical sections or front-end bidding documents instead.
  9. The correct answer is B (General conditions for standard terms and supplementary conditions for project-specific terms): General conditions establish standard contract terms the owner applies broadly across many projects, while supplementary conditions modify or add terms specific to the individual project at hand. Technical specifications and drawings address physical work requirements, and the bid form and addenda serve bidding purposes rather than defining general versus project-specific contract terms.
  10. The correct answer is D (Part 1, General, which addresses administrative and procedural requirements): Part 1, General, of a SectionFormat specification section addresses administrative and procedural information such as reference standards, quality assurance, and delivery and storage requirements. Part 2, Products, addresses material composition, and Part 3, Execution, addresses installation, so this administrative content belongs in Part 1 rather than the other parts.
  11. Part 1 covers general administrative items, Part 3 covers execution and installation, and the bid form is unrelated to material specification content.
  12. The correct answer is C (Reduced substitution requests but potentially higher pricing from limited competition): A proprietary, single-source specification typically reduces the number of qualified suppliers, which can reduce substitution requests but also reduce competitive pricing pressure, potentially raising cost. It does not automatically increase competition, guarantee compliance with reference standards, or eliminate the need for a submittal on that furnishing item.

It does not relieve the contractor of coordination responsibility, and it does not automatically convert the specification into a performance specification.
  14. Guaranteeing fabricator calculations and approving labor rates or purchase costs also fall outside this review's purpose.

The correct answer is B (Approved as noted, allowing fabrication to proceed with the noted correction): Approved as noted is the appropriate stamp when a submittal substantially matches design intent but requires a minor correction, allowing fabrication to proceed once the noted item is addressed. Rejected or revise and resubmit would unnecessarily halt fabrication for a minor issue, and a plain approved stamp would omit the needed correction note.
  16. The correct answer is B (Revise and resubmit, since the submittal requires substantial rework before approval): Revise and resubmit is used when a submittal deviates significantly from the specified requirements and needs substantial rework before it can proceed, requiring an entirely new submission for review. Approved and approved as noted both indicate the submittal is acceptable as is or with only minor notes, which does not fit this scenario.
  17.   18. The correct answer is C (A request for information, submitted to resolve a document ambiguity): A request for information is the standard mechanism a contractor uses to seek written clarification of an ambiguity or apparent conflict between contract documents, such as conflicting grading and layout dimensions. A construction change directive authorizes work, a substitution request proposes an alternate product, and a bid protest challenges bidding procedures.
  19. The correct answer is D (Provide a clear, documented clarification or direction resolving the field conflict): The landscape architect's RFI response should provide a clear, documented clarification or direction that resolves the specific field conflict described, giving the contractor a basis to proceed. It should not unilaterally authorize a price change without documentation, halt the entire project, or direct an unrelated bid protest process.
  20. The correct answers are B and D: An RFI seeks clarification of a genuine ambiguity or conflict in the contract documents and creates a documented record of the question and its answer for future reference. It does not authorize the contractor to unilaterally change the contract sum, and it has no bearing on the contractor's bonding requirements.
  21. The correct answer is C (It documents review status and timing to help keep the project on schedule): A submittal log tracks review status and timing for every shop drawing, product data sheet, and sample, helping the team monitor whether submittals are keeping pace with the construction schedule. It does not substitute for bonds, the certificate of substantial completion, or the landscape architect's ongoing site observation obligations.
  22. Approving a nonconforming sample, issuing an unwarranted construction change directive, or ignoring the deviation would all fail to enforce the specification.
  23.   24. The correct answer is C (To establish a basis for reviewing monthly applications for payment): A schedule of values breaks the lump-sum contract price into line items that serve as the basis for reviewing and approving each monthly application for payment against actual progress.   25. A construction change directive is used when agreement has not yet been reached, an RFI seeks clarification without changing the contract, and an addendum applies only during bidding.
  26. A change order requires prior agreement on price and time, an addendum applies only before contract award, and a certificate of substantial completion is unrelated to directing new work.
  27. A construction change directive is used during construction, not before the contract is signed, and a change order always involves both parties' agreement and signatures.
  28.   31. Authorizing requests without owner review, ignoring the line item, or transferring unused funds to contractor profit would all be inappropriate handling of contingency funds.
  32. The correct answer is D (That the materials are suitably protected, insured, and clearly documented on site): For stored materials to be included in an application for payment, the contractor typically must show the materials are properly protected, insured, and clearly documented as belonging to the project. Materials do not need to already be installed, retainage is not automatically waived, and a construction change directive is unrelated to stored material documentation.

  34. Setting the contractor's internal labor rates or insurance premiums are business decisions that fall outside the design professional's role.
  35. Means and methods supervision belongs to the contractor, CM-at-risk describes a different delivery method, and bid administration occurs before contract award.

  37.   40.   46. The correct answer is A (Retainage, withheld from progress payments until specified conditions are met): The amount an owner withholds from progress payments as a financial safeguard against incomplete or defective work is called retainage, commonly around five to ten percent, released once specified conditions are met. This differs from a performance bond held by a surety, a contingency allowance for unforeseen conditions, and liquidated damages assessed for late completion.
  47. The correct answer is B (Final completion, when all contract obligations have been fully satisfied): Once all punch list items are complete and the landscape architect confirms the work is fully finished, this milestone is called final completion, representing full satisfaction of the contract obligations.   48.   49. The correct answer is D (The guarantee or warranty period, covering defects discovered after completion): The one-year period following substantial completion during which the contractor must repair or replace defective materials and workmanship at no cost is commonly called the guarantee or warranty period. The bidding period occurs before contract award, retainage involves withheld payment rather than a time period, and the pre-construction period occurs before construction begins.
  50. It does not unconditionally and immediately release all lien rights, does not replace a performance bond, and has no effect on the warranty period.

The correct answers are B and D: Final, unconditional lien waivers confirming payment to subcontractors and suppliers, along with completed record drawings and operation and maintenance manuals, are commonly required before an owner releases final retainage. A new bid bond or an updated invitation to bid relate to a future procurement, not to closing out the current contract's retainage.
  98. The correct answer is D (Withhold recommendation for final payment until the missing documentation is obtained): Discovering that required close-out warranty documentation is missing from a subcontractor should lead the landscape architect to withhold the recommendation for final payment until that documentation is obtained. Recommending payment anyway, contacting the original apparent low bidder for an unrelated document, or issuing a new addendum to rebid the scope would not resolve the missing documentation properly.
  99. The correct answer is B (5 to 10 percent of each progress payment): A commonly used retainage range on many public construction projects, subject to the applicable statute, is approximately five to ten percent of each progress payment. Fifty to sixty percent, less than one percent, and ninety to ninety-five percent would all be unusually and unrealistically high or low retainage figures for standard construction contracts.
  100. The correct answer is B (To improve contractor cash flow once a project demonstrates satisfactory progress): A reduced-retainage provision at a stated completion milestone, such as fifty percent, is generally intended to improve the contractor's cash flow once the project has demonstrated satisfactory progress. It does not eliminate warranty obligations, does not automatically trigger additional change orders, and does not replace the later certificate of substantial completion.
  101. The correct answer is D (Earlier constructability feedback and cost estimating input during design development): Early CM-at-risk involvement typically provides earlier constructability feedback and cost estimating input during design development, before the design is fully finalized. It does not eliminate competitive subcontractor bidding for individual trade packages, does not guarantee a lower total cost without accounting for scope, and does not remove the landscape architect's construction administration responsibilities.
  102. The correct answer is B (The role may shift toward representing the design-build entity or the owner, depending on the contract): Under design-build delivery, the landscape architect's role may shift toward representing either the design-build entity itself or the owner, depending on how the specific contract is structured. Construction administration duties do not disappear, design-build does not require holding a contractor's license, and submittal review remains a necessary part of construction administration.
  103. The correct answers are C and D: A construction manager typically joins the project team earlier, during design, and the arrangement often includes a guaranteed maximum price established before the full scope goes out for subcontractor bidding. CM-at-risk does not eliminate subcontractor bidding on individual trades, and design does not need to be one hundred percent complete before this type of contract begins.
  104. The correct answer is D (Prioritizing maximum owner design control and independent design oversight): Prioritizing maximum owner design control and independent design oversight would most likely favor design-bid-build, since it keeps the design professional's role separate from and independent of the builder. Prioritizing schedule speed, single-point responsibility, or early contractor cost input would instead point toward design-build or CM-at-risk rather than design-bid-build.
  105. The correct answer is D (Allowing multiple manufacturers to compete by meeting a defined performance outcome): A performance specification for a stormwater filtration system allows multiple manufacturers to compete by demonstrating they can meet a defined performance outcome, rather than restricting the field to one product. It does not restrict bidders to a single manufacturer, does not eliminate product data submittal requirements, and does not guarantee the lowest possible cost regardless of quality.
  106. The correct answer is B (To reserve a set dollar amount for items whose final selection is not yet made): A cash allowance reserves a set dollar amount within the contract for items, such as decorative benches, whose final selection has not yet been made at the time of bidding. It does not eliminate the schedule of values, does not automatically waive retainage on that line item, and does not remove the requirement for a submittal once the item is selected.
  107. The correct answer is D (The overage is processed through a change order adjusting the contract sum): When actual furnishings cost exceeds a stated cash allowance, the overage is typically processed through a change order that formally adjusts the contract sum to reflect the additional cost. The overage is not simply absorbed by the contractor, it does not void the entire allowance provision, and it is not deducted from the contractor's payment bond.
  108. The correct answer is C (Issue a formal addendum documenting the clarification for all bidders of record): A bidding question whose answer changes the scope shown in the drawings must be incorporated through a formal addendum documenting the clarification for all bidders of record, preserving fairness in the competitive process. Answering only one bidder privately, waiting until after bid opening, or modifying drawings without notice would all compromise the integrity of the bidding process.
  109. The correct answer is A (Request bid verification from the apparent low bidder per the bidding document procedures): When a significant gap between bids raises concern about a possible error, an appropriate step is to request bid verification from the apparent low bidder according to the procedures established in the bidding documents. Automatically disqualifying the bidder, awarding the contract without further review, or arbitrarily increasing the contract sum would all bypass proper bid verification procedures.
  110. The correct answer is B (Best value considers qualifications and experience alongside price rather than price alone): A key consideration favoring best-value selection is that it weighs qualifications and relevant experience alongside price, rather than relying on price alone as the sole selection factor. Best value does not always result in a lower total price, does not eliminate bonding requirements, and does not automatically disqualify the lowest responsive and responsible bidder.
  111. The correct answers are B and C: Addenda formally modify or clarify the bidding documents before bids are due, and they are properly distributed to every bidder of record rather than only the bidder who asked the original question. Addenda are not issued after the contract has been executed, and they do not replace the bid bond requirement on public construction projects.
  112. The correct answer is B (To help the owner gauge budget adequacy before bids are received): A pre-bid engineer's estimate of probable construction cost helps the owner gauge whether the available budget is adequate before actual bids are received. It does not set the exact final contract price, replace the schedule of values used during construction, or typically disqualify a bid that differs from the estimate.
  113. The correct answer is B (Negotiate scope reductions or value engineering options with the apparent low bidder): When bids substantially exceed the available budget, a common approach before rejecting all bids is to negotiate scope reductions or value engineering options with the apparent low bidder to bring the project within budget. Doubling the budget without discussion, awarding over budget without approval, or skipping review of alternates would not represent sound project budget management.
  114. The correct answer is A (The right to reject any or all bids as stated in the bidding documents): The instructions to bidders typically reserve the owner's right to reject any or all bids, which allows the owner to decline every bid received, such as when pricing substantially exceeds the available budget. This right does not extend to retroactively lowering retainage, awarding to a non-bidder, or eliminating the performance bond requirement after award.
  115. The correct answer is A (A bidder demonstrating the capability, integrity, and resources to perform the work): A responsible bidder is one that demonstrates the capability, integrity, financial resources, and experience needed to properly perform the contracted work. It does not simply refer to the lowest total bid amount, attendance order at a pre-bid meeting, or the number of requests for information submitted, which relate to responsiveness or other factors instead.
  116. The correct answer is C (A bidder whose bid conforms to all material requirements of the bidding documents): A responsive bidder is one whose bid conforms to all material requirements of the bidding documents, such as including a required bid bond, rather than simply being the lowest dollar amount. Prior project experience and the timing of bid submission relate to different evaluation factors, not the definition of responsiveness itself.
  117. Personal preference, the order bids were physically received, or a bidder's willingness to waive a required bond would all be improper bases for a contract award recommendation.
  118. The correct answers are A and C: A responsive bidder submits a bid conforming to the material requirements of the bidding documents, and a responsible bidder demonstrates the capability, integrity, and resources needed to perform the contract. Responsiveness is not defined solely by submitting the lowest dollar amount, and a responsible bidder is not exempt from applicable bonding or insurance requirements.
  119. The correct answer is A (Requiring questions in writing by a stated deadline, answered through addenda to all bidders): Standard practice requires bidders to submit questions in writing by a stated deadline, with answers distributed to all bidders through a formal addendum, ensuring fairness across the competitive bidding process. Allowing only verbal, privately answered questions, accepting questions after the deadline, or prohibiting questions altogether would all undermine fair and transparent bidding.
  120. The correct answer is B (That the substitute product is equal to or better than the specified product in relevant respects): Under a typical substitution procedure, the contractor must demonstrate that the proposed substitute product is equal to or better than the specified product in the relevant performance and quality respects. Simply being less expensive, having been used on an unrelated project, or being the general contractor's personal preference would not, by themselves, satisfy the substitution requirement.
  121. The correct answer is C (Deny the substitution request and require the contractor to provide the specified product): When a proposed substitution fails to meet the specified performance criteria, such as required permeability, the appropriate response is to deny the substitution request and require the contractor to provide the originally specified product. Approving it anyway, ignoring the request, or automatically increasing the contract sum would all fail to protect the specified project performance requirements.
  122. The correct answer is A (Preconstruction meeting, submittal review and RFIs, substantial completion, final completion): The general sequence of construction administration activities runs from the preconstruction meeting, through submittal review and RFI responses during construction, to substantial completion, and finally to final completion once all obligations are satisfied. The other sequences place these milestones out of their logical order, such as final completion occurring before the work it is meant to conclude.
  123. The correct answer is A (The ridgelines and topographic high points that separate directions of surface flow): The watershed boundary is defined by ridgelines and other topographic high points, not by any legal or administrative line. Surface runoff always flows away from these high points toward lower ground on either side, which is what actually separates one drainage area from another. Property lines, parking edges, and zoning boundaries can cross a true watershed divide with no hydrologic relationship to it. Delineating the divide correctly from contour data is the first step before any runoff calculation.
  124. The correct answer is C (The divide follows a continuous line connecting points of highest elevation between two basins): A watershed divide is drawn by connecting the highest elevation points that separate two adjacent drainage basins, following the ridgeline shown on a topographic map. Water on either side of that divide necessarily flows toward two different downstream discharge points, which is the defining hydrologic test of a true divide. A road centerline or a small parking lot's grading has no inherent relationship to where a topographic divide actually sits. Confirming the divide against contours, not assumed lines, avoids errors in the drainage area used for later calculations.
  125. The correct answer is A (5.4 cfs): Using the Rational Method, Q equals C times i times A, so Q equals 0.90 times 3.0 in/hr times 2.0 acres, which equals 5.4 cfs. Skipping the intensity term and multiplying only C times A gives the incorrect 1.8 cfs shown as a distractor. Skipping the runoff coefficient and multiplying only i times A gives the incorrect 6.0 cfs shown as another distractor. Every term in the formula, including units of acres and in/hr, must be included exactly once to produce the correct peak flow.
  126. The correct answer is B (A conventional concrete sidewalk with a solid, non-jointed surface): A conventional concrete sidewalk has a solid, continuous, non-jointed surface that sheds nearly all rainfall as runoff, which is the defining characteristic of an impervious surface for Rational Method purposes. Undisturbed forest floor, unsealed gravel, and unmowed meadow all allow substantial infiltration and carry much lower runoff coefficients. Correctly classifying each surface type is essential because the composite C value used in Q equals C times i times A depends directly on getting this classification right. Misclassifying a pervious surface as impervious inflates the calculated peak flow.
  127. The correct answer is B (Replacing a portion of standard asphalt parking with permeable interlocking pavers): Replacing standard asphalt with permeable interlocking pavers directly converts a fully impervious surface, with a C value near 0.90 to 0.95, into a surface that infiltrates a portion of incoming rainfall and carries a much lower effective coefficient. Increasing slope, adding another asphalt layer, or repainting striping all leave the surface fully impervious and do nothing to reduce the weighted C value. Because the composite C is an area-weighted average, converting even a portion of impervious area to a lower-C surface lowers the overall site value. This is a core low impact development strategy for meeting a stormwater ordinance's runoff limits.
  128. The correct answers are A and B: a wooded area with undisturbed, permeable soil typically carries a low C value in the range of about 0.05 to 0.20, and a built-up roof is a fully impervious surface typically assigned a C value near 0.90 to 0.95. Turf lawn is a much lower-runoff surface than a roof and typically falls closer to 0.15 to 0.30, well below 0.80. Gravel surfaces vary with compaction and binder but are consistently lower than bare asphalt, so treating them as identical is incorrect. These reference ranges are standard published Rational Method values used across most jurisdictional stormwater manuals.

Dropping the intensity term and multiplying only C times A gives the incorrect 1.0 cfs shown as a distractor. Dropping the runoff coefficient and multiplying only i times A gives 20.0 cfs, an order of magnitude too high, shown as another distractor. Every factor in the equation must be applied together, with area expressed in acres and intensity in inches per hour, to arrive at the correct peak flow in cubic feet per second.
  130. The correct answer is D (Point D): Point D sits farthest into the upper right corner of the grading plan, crossing the highest-numbered contour line shown and therefore representing the point at the greatest elevation and the longest overland travel distance from the low corner. A longer flow path to that point, combined with the flat, evenly spaced contour interval shown across the site, produces a longer time of concentration than any of the other three points. Points A, B, and C sit closer to the low corner or lower on the slope and cross lower-numbered contour lines, meaning water reaches those locations more quickly. Correctly reading the contour pattern to identify the point at the top of the watershed is the first step in tracing the longest flow path for a time of concentration calculation.
  131. D culvert invert is set to match the low point of existing grade along the drainage path so that flow can pass through it without being dammed. Points B, C, and A sit progressively closer to the upper right corner of the plan and cross higher-numbered contour lines, placing them at higher elevations than Point D. Correctly identifying the lowest point on the plan is the first step in confirming that a proposed culvert invert elevation is consistent with existing topography before final sizing calculations proceed.
  132. The correct answer is A (Intensity is expressed in inches per hour and is read from an IDF curve for the site's region): Rainfall intensity, i, in the Rational Method is expressed in inches per hour and is obtained from an intensity duration frequency, or IDF, curve specific to the project's geographic region and selected design storm. Measuring intensity in gallons per minute would confuse a volumetric flow unit with a rainfall rate and is not how the Rational Method defines the term. Intensity varies significantly by region and by storm duration, so it is never a single fixed national constant. It also should not be approximated as a flat 24-hour average, since the Rational Method specifically uses peak intensity matched to the time of concentration.
  133. The correct answer is C (Point C): Point C sits nearest the upper right corner of the grading plan, along the highest-numbered contour line shown, placing it at the ridge line that forms the upper boundary of the delineated watershed. If a drainage area boundary were mistakenly drawn to exclude this high point, the contributing area used in the Rational Method would be understated, producing an incorrect and undersized peak flow calculation. Points A, B, and D sit at lower elevations, closer to the low corner of the plan, and are clearly within the interior of the watershed rather than along its boundary. Correctly tracing the ridge line against the contour pattern shown is essential before any watershed area is used in a downstream sizing calculation.
  134. The correct answer is A (0.425): The composite, area-weighted runoff coefficient is calculated as the sum of each sub-area's C times its area, divided by the total area: (3 acres times 0.95, plus 7 acres times 0.20), divided by 10 acres. That is (2.85 plus 1.4) divided by 10, which equals 4.25 divided by 10, or 0.425. Taking a simple, unweighted average of the two C values instead would incorrectly produce 0.575, ignoring that the lawn area is more than twice as large as the roof area. Area weighting is essential whenever sub-areas of different sizes and different cover types are combined into a single composite coefficient.
  135. The correct answer is B (Pretreat sediment first, then route flow through a biofiltration practice before final discharge): Pretreating sediment first, then routing flow through a biofiltration practice before final discharge, reflects a proper treatment train that removes coarse solids before finer water quality treatment occurs. Discharging runoff directly to the storm sewer with no pretreatment, or routing everything through an underground vault with no vegetated treatment component, both skip the biological and filtration processes that sustainable water quality practice relies on. Combining sanitary sewage with stormwater in a shared pipe is also improper and is prohibited under standard separate sewer system design. A properly sequenced treatment train improves overall pollutant removal beyond what any single practice provides alone.
  136. The correct answers are A and B: poorly draining clay soils generally favor a filtration or detention approach rather than deep infiltration, since water cannot pass through the soil profile quickly enough to prevent prolonged ponding, and a high seasonal groundwater table can preclude a deep infiltration practice at that location to avoid groundwater contamination or facility failure. Soil infiltration rate is one of the primary site conditions used to select an appropriate BMP and cannot be ignored once a generic BMP type has been chosen from a manual. Different BMP types perform very differently depending on the underlying native soil, so infiltration testing is a required step in BMP selection.
  137. The correct answer is C (A subsurface infiltration or detention system placed beneath the plaza paving): A subsurface infiltration or detention system placed beneath the plaza paving allows the site to meet its water quality volume requirement without consuming any additional surface land area, which is the defining constraint in a dense urban context. A large surface retention pond or a constructed wetland both require substantially more open land than is available on a tightly constrained urban forecourt. A regional detention basin located miles away also does not address the on-site treatment requirement typically imposed by the jurisdiction. Subsurface systems are a standard solution when surface area for a conventional BMP simply does not exist.
